Preserve current trusted v1 safety, but remove unnecessary user-facing restrictions: make fixed motion presets optional/simple-mode instead of the core UX, support richer freeform animation intent capture, keep repeated asset reuse visible as counts not duplicate spam, persist compatible lane/provenance metadata, and prepare the data/render contracts for future schema-sequence and generated Remotion module lanes without executing untrusted code in the browser.         Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: First Practical Generated Sequence Unlock

## Overview
The current Reigh implementation already has the trusted v1 spine the architecture doc wants to preserve: `SequenceCreatorPanel` calls `ai-generate-sequence`, validates returned data-only drafts, previews them through existing Remotion components, and inserts ordinary timeline clips through `sequence-drafts`. Timeline persistence already preserves `params`, `pool_id`, `clip_order`, `source_uuid`, and `generation` metadata through `TimelineConfig` round-trips. The first practical unlock should therefore stay narrow: improve authoring intent and provenance for trusted v1, remove the feeling that fixed motion presets are the core UX, keep repeated asset use readable as counts, and add render-router safety for future `remotion_module` lane metadata without enabling browser code execution.

Existing dirty files include `.megaplan/schemas/*`, `SequenceCreatorPanel.tsx`, `SequenceParamEditor.tsx`, and `ClipPanel.sequence.test.tsx`. Treat those as current workspace state and do not touch `.megaplan` schema dirtiness.

## Phase 1: Trusted V1 Authoring UX And Request Contract

### Step 1: Add explicit intent capture to `SequenceCreatorPanel` (`src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/SequenceCreatorPanel.tsx`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Introduce a small local intent state beside `prompt` and `editPrompt`, for example `animationIntent`, with a freeform textarea or compact field for motion, timing, reuse, style, and structure notes.
2. Keep fixed motion options as simple-mode guidance only. Do not require users to pick a preset before generation; the primary action remains prompt-driven generation.
3. Send the intent as a structured request field to `ai-generate-sequence`, e.g. `animation_intent: { freeform: animationIntent }`, while keeping the existing `allowed_clip_types` trusted v1 allowlist.
4. In edit mode, include intent in `edit_context` or the top-level body so edits can say things like “make this more kinetic, repeat asset A three times” without relying on the `image-jump.mode` selector.

### Step 2: Preserve lane/provenance metadata on inserted trusted clips (`src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/SequenceCreatorPanel.tsx`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/sequence-drafts.ts`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Extend `buildSequenceGenerationMetadata()` so inserted/replaced trusted clips get `generation.sequence_lane: 'trusted_v1'` plus the existing `sequence_creator` prompt/name/draft index metadata.
2. Include captured freeform intent in provenance, e.g. `generation.sequence_creator.intent`, but do not persist any code, imports, raw URLs, or animation refs.
3. Add/adjust a focused `sequence-drafts` or `SequenceCreatorPanel` test proving the inserted clip round-trips with `generation.sequence_lane === 'trusted_v1'` through `rowsToConfig()`.

### Step 3: Keep repeated asset reuse visible as counts (`src/tools/video-editor/components/PropertiesPanel/SequenceParamEditor.tsx`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Preserve the existing counted-chip behavior for repeated asset keys and ensure the editable input does not duplicate asset labels as spam.
2. Add a focused test for `imageAssetKeys: ['asset-a', 'asset-a', 'asset-b']` showing two displayed assets with `asset-a x2`, while the underlying params still preserve the repeated key array for render materialization.
3. If the current input drops duplicates when edited, keep that behavior only for manual re-entry; generated/model-provided duplicates should remain visible and persisted.

## Phase 2: Trusted Draft Validation And Edge Prompting

### Step 4: Accept safe freeform animation intent without relaxing trusted output (`src/tools/video-editor/sequences/validation.ts`, `supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence/sequence-validation.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Keep the draft output shape data-only: `clipType`, `hold`, `params` only.
2. Do not allow top-level `animation`, `entrance`, `exit`, `transition`, `imports`, `source`, `code`, raw URLs, or arbitrary component refs in trusted drafts.
3. Add an optional trusted metadata field only if needed for provenance after validation; otherwise keep intent outside the draft and attach it at insertion time.
4. If adding any schema surface for lane metadata, type it narrowly as `trusted_v1 | schema_sequence | remotion_module` at Reigh-local clip metadata boundaries, with `artifact_id` optional except where router safety requires it.

### Step 5: Teach `ai-generate-sequence` about intent while keeping validation closed (`supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence/templates.ts`, `supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence/index.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add `animationIntent?: unknown` to `BuildGenerateSequenceMessagesInput` and include it in the JSON user message.
2. Update the system prompt to treat freeform intent as guidance for selecting trusted clip types, timing, asset reuse, and safe string params, not as permission to emit code or unsupported animation references.
3. Include request-body plumbing from `body.animation_intent` to `buildGenerateSequenceMessages()`.
4. Update edge tests to assert the Anthropic user message contains `animation_intent` and that invalid code/raw URL drafts are still rejected.

## Phase 3: Future Lane Data/Render Contracts Without Browser Code Execution

### Step 6: Add generated-lane render-router safety (`src/tools/video-editor/lib/renderRouter.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Extend `RouterClipShape` with optional `generation?: { sequence_lane?: unknown; artifact_id?: unknown; artifact_version?: unknown; compiler_version?: unknown }`.
2. Add `isGeneratedRemotionModuleClip()` and inspect `clip.generation.sequence_lane` before theme-package or native clip-type checks.
3. Return a blocked/error decision for `sequence_lane === 'remotion_module'` with missing, empty, or non-string `artifact_id`; do not return `client`.
4. Route valid module-lane clips to the worker route (`banodoco`) with a distinct reason such as `generated_remotion_module`.
5. Keep unknown `clipType` without module lane on the existing client loud-placeholder fallback.

### Step 7: Keep persistence additive and schema-compatible (`src/tools/video-editor/types/index.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/serialize.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/timeline-data.ts`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Confirm no migration is required: existing clips may omit `clip.generation` entirely.
2. If adding local types, define additive sequence generation metadata without changing `TimelineConfig` storage fields away from config plus asset registry.
3. Do not hand-edit generated shared schema artifacts. If the local shared `@banodoco/timeline-schema` source cannot already represent the optional generation fields needed for this unlock, update its source/generator in the shared package and regenerate outputs in the owning package; otherwise leave generated artifacts untouched.

## Phase 4: Focused Tests And Verification

### Step 8: Add focused unit coverage
**Scope:** Medium
1. `src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/SequenceCreatorPanel.test.tsx`: request body includes freeform intent, trusted allowlist remains present, inserted metadata includes `trusted_v1` provenance.
2. `src/tools/video-editor/components/PropertiesPanel/ClipPanel.sequence.test.tsx` or a new `SequenceParamEditor` test: repeated asset keys render as counts, not duplicate chips.
3. `supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence/index.test.ts`: prompt includes intent but output remains closed and unsafe drafts are rejected.
4. `src/tools/video-editor/lib/renderRouter.test.ts`: valid `remotion_module` lane routes to worker by lane metadata; missing `artifact_id` blocks; registered theme clip with module lane still uses generated-module reason; unknown clipType without lane remains client fallback; `trusted_v1` and `schema_sequence` do not trigger module routing.
5. `src/tools/video-editor/lib/timeline-data.test.ts`: lane/provenance metadata round-trips when present, and no-generation legacy clips still round-trip.

### Step 9: Run cheapest validation first, then broader checks
**Scope:** Small
1. Run targeted UI/editor tests with `npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.config.ts src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/SequenceCreatorPanel.test.tsx src/tools/video-editor/lib/renderRouter.test.ts src/tools/video-editor/lib/timeline-data.test.ts`.
2. Run edge unit coverage with `npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.edge.config.ts supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence/index.test.ts`.
3. Run `npm run lint -- --quiet` or the repo’s normal `npm run lint` after the focused tests pass.
4. Finish with `npm test` if runtime allows.

## Execution Order
1. Start with type/test additions for router lane safety, because this is a hard safety boundary and independent of UI copy.
2. Add SequenceCreator request/provenance plumbing next, keeping trusted v1 as the default.
3. Update edge prompt plumbing and tests after the client request shape is known.
4. Finish with repeated-asset display tests and any small UI adjustments needed by current dirty state.
5. Avoid `.megaplan/` schema files entirely unless the harness itself changes them.

## Validation Order
1. Targeted render-router tests first.
2. Targeted SequenceCreator and SequenceParamEditor tests second.
3. Edge function unit tests third.
4. Lint/typecheck and broader `npm test` last.

        {
  "agent-chat-rendering": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-007",
      "concern": "removing the duplicate assistant turn may hide message_user text if the frontend only renders assistant-role turns as chat bubbles.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "refactor-ai-timeline-agent-20260326-0157"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "agent-chat-undo-integration": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-018",
      "concern": "agent chat edits arrive via query invalidation/realtime polling and will bypass the undo stack because those paths are marked skiphistory.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-undo-redo-history-20260326-2240"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "agent-tool-utils": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-040",
      "concern": "agent-tool-utils: step 6 moves `isrecord` out of `llm/messages.ts` without preserving that module's current export surface for `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, so the refactor plan can break four existing imports unless it adds a re-export or rewires those callers.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "cleanup-and-quality-pass-on-20260404-0138"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-041",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the actual export surface in `supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/llm/messages.ts` and its consumers. step 6 says to 'remove local `isrecord`' from `messages.ts` and import it from `utils.ts`, but without also preserving `messages.ts` as an exporter or rewiring `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, that change would break the module graph immediately.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "cleanup-and-quality-pass-on-20260404-0138"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-055",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the render-gating cleanup plan against remotion's runtime exports in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/cancel-render.js:46-70`. `cancelrender()` stores an error and throws to abort the render; it is not a benign cleanup for an abandoned `delayrender` handle, so step 2's instruction to call `cancelrender(handle)` on unmount is technically wrong and would abort preview or export with an error.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "add-audio-reactive-effects-to-20260406-0440"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-056",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked how remotion itself acquires delay handles in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/iframe.js:8-18`. remotion's own components create the handle synchronously with `usestate(() => delayrender(...))`; the plan says 'calls delayrender() on mount', which reads like an effect-time action and leaves the initial-frame blocking behavior underspecified compared with the actual library pattern.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "add-audio-reactive-effects-to-20260406-0440"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-057",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new `useaudioparam()` sketch against `src/tools/video-editor/effects/compileeffect.tsx:1-2`. the plan defines it in terms of `interpolate(audio[binding.source], ...)` but does not explicitly import `interpolate` into the new hook file or simplify it to straight arithmetic, so the helper's implementation contract is still looser than it needs to be.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "add-audio-reactive-effects-to-20260406-0440"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-070",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked revised step 2.4 against `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useclipresize.ts:76-100`. the plan still says to clamp audio by writing back to `action.end`, but the current hook treats the callback `action` as input data and then creates fresh row actions from it. mutating that caller-owned object is unnecessary and risks coupling the resize hook to outside references instead of using a local `clampedend` variable.",

    {
      "id": "DEBT-076",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the no-duration-fetch decision against `src/tools/travel-between-images/hooks/video/useshotfinalvideos.ts:19-37`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/config-utils.ts:17-35`, and `src/tools/video-editor/compositions/visualclip.tsx:114-123`. the repository still derives clip length from `to - from`, and the final-video query still provides no duration metadata, so the revised plan still cannot prove that `to: endtime - starttime` is safe for shorter source media; a short final video can still be asked to play longer than its actual content.",

      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the hand-built replacement clip against the existing insert path in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useassetmanagement.ts:201-252,262-271` and serialization in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts:210-216`. the revised `timelineeditor` sketch still bypasses current insertion invariants such as manual-track geometry defaults and explicit `cliporder` updates, so replacement clips on manual visual tracks will not be initialized the same way as other inserted video clips and may serialize in a different per-track order.",

      "id": "DEBT-089",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new `pinnedshotgroupsoverride` design is still underspecified against `materializedata()`. `assembletimelinedata()` computes `stablesignature` from the config it receives, so if the executor literally follows step 2.4 and overwrites `pinnedshotgroups` on an already-materialized `timelinedata` before `commitdata()`, the saved config and the cached `stablesignature` will diverge unless the data is rebuilt from the updated config.",

    {
      "id": "DEBT-038",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the current callers of `isrecord` from `llm/messages.ts`. `index.ts` uses it to validate `rawsession`, `db.ts` uses it on db rows, `tool-calls.ts` uses it on parsed tool-call payloads and nested `function` objects, and `selectedclips.ts` uses it while normalizing attachments, so preserving that import contract matters to four different call paths.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "cleanup-and-quality-pass-on-20260404-0138"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-095",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the current `commitdata()` and history pipeline treat each `applyedit()` as its own commit boundary. because step 16 proposes a post-commit reconcile edit after deletions, the actual callers here matter: without `skiphistory` or transaction coalescing, one user action will produce two history events and likely two saves.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-096",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: `handleassetdrop()` and the external-drop wrapper are not pure builders; they immediately mutate editor state and call `applyedit()` per asset. that means the plan cannot literally reuse those callers for atomic shot-drop creation without first extracting a lower-level helper, or it will violate its own \"single applyedit\" requirement.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-111",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the actual commit/history callers around step 18. `commitdata()` emits `beforecommit` and schedules save on every edit, and `usetimelinehistory.onbeforecommit()` pushes undo entries per commit boundary, so a reconcile-after-delete `applyedit` still fragments one user action into multiple history/save operations unless the plan also changes those caller contracts.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-115",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the multi-drag callers in `useclipdrag`. the 'new track' grouped-drag branch still routes through `buildconfigfromdragresult()` and `applyedit({ type: 'config' })`, so the revised plan still does not fully handle every caller shape for grouped moves once pinned-group metadata becomes part of the timeline state.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-130",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the `usepinnedgroupsync()` call site in `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx:479` against the proposed context-based interaction guard. because sync is called directly from `timelineeditor`, routing its guard through new editor-context plumbing is not the most direct fit for the actual caller and depends on extra provider/type changes that the step does not enumerate.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "refactor-pinned-shot-groups-20260408-0508"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-138",

        "id": "DEBT-041",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the actual export surface in `supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/llm/messages.ts` and its consumers. step 6 says to 'remove local `isrecord`' from `messages.ts` and import it from `utils.ts`, but without also preserving `messages.ts` as an exporter or rewiring `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, that change would break the module graph immediately.",

        "id": "DEBT-055",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the render-gating cleanup plan against remotion's runtime exports in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/cancel-render.js:46-70`. `cancelrender()` stores an error and throws to abort the render; it is not a benign cleanup for an abandoned `delayrender` handle, so step 2's instruction to call `cancelrender(handle)` on unmount is technically wrong and would abort preview or export with an error.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "add-audio-reactive-effects-to-20260406-0440"
        ]
      },

        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "add-audio-reactive-effects-to-20260406-0440"

        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the no-duration-fetch decision against `src/tools/travel-between-images/hooks/video/useshotfinalvideos.ts:19-37`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/config-utils.ts:17-35`, and `src/tools/video-editor/compositions/visualclip.tsx:114-123`. the repository still derives clip length from `to - from`, and the final-video query still provides no duration metadata, so the revised plan still cannot prove that `to: endtime - starttime` is safe for shorter source media; a short final video can still be asked to play longer than its actual content.",

        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the hand-built replacement clip against the existing insert path in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useassetmanagement.ts:201-252,262-271` and serialization in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts:210-216`. the revised `timelineeditor` sketch still bypasses current insertion invariants such as manual-track geometry defaults and explicit `cliporder` updates, so replacement clips on manual visual tracks will not be initialized the same way as other inserted video clips and may serialize in a different per-track order.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,

      {
        "id": "DEBT-089",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new `pinnedshotgroupsoverride` design is still underspecified against `materializedata()`. `assembletimelinedata()` computes `stablesignature` from the config it receives, so if the executor literally follows step 2.4 and overwrites `pinnedshotgroups` on an already-materialized `timelinedata` before `commitdata()`, the saved config and the cached `stablesignature` will diverge unless the data is rebuilt from the updated config.",

        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 5.1 claims `updatepinnedgroupwithchildren` is 'currently imported by useswitchtofinalvideo.ts:357'. verified at useswitchtofinalvideo.ts:71-130 that this function is actually **defined locally** in useswitchtofinalvideo.ts, not imported from pinned-group-projection.ts. the real imports from pinned-group-projection at :5-8 are `buildgroupfromchildren` and `replacegroupchildren`, both of which are already in step 5's delete list. the plan's characterization is factually wrong (delete list shouldn't include updatepinnedgroupwithchildren as a pinned-group-projection export), but the substantive intent still works: once `buildgroupfromchildren`/`replacegroupchildren` are deleted, the local helper won't compile and step 11.2's 'inline soft-tag construction' rewrite removes it. minor wording error, not a blocking issue.",

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Idea:
Implement the first practical unlock for maximum-power animation sequences in Reigh, based on docs/generated-sequences-architecture.md. Preserve current trusted v1 safety, but remove unnecessary user-facing restrictions: make fixed motion presets optional/simple-mode instead of the core UX, support richer freeform animation intent capture, keep repeated asset reuse visible as counts not duplicate spam, persist compatible lane/provenance metadata, and prepare the data/render contracts for future schema-sequence and generated Remotion module lanes without executing untrusted code in the browser.         Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: First Practical Generated Sequence Unlock

## Overview
The current Reigh implementation already has the trusted v1 spine the architecture doc wants to preserve: `SequenceCreatorPanel` calls `ai-generate-sequence`, validates returned data-only drafts, previews them through existing Remotion components, and inserts ordinary timeline clips through `sequence-drafts`. Timeline persistence already preserves `params`, `pool_id`, `clip_order`, `source_uuid`, and `generation` metadata through `TimelineConfig` round-trips.

The critique shows one root-cause correction: render-route safety cannot be implemented only in `renderRouter.ts`, because `decideRenderRoute()` currently has no production callers and the live render button path goes through `useRenderState()` to `useClientRender()`. The revised plan therefore makes the live render entrypoint enforce the router decision, with an explicit blocked route for malformed generated-module clips. This is not extra product scope; it is required to satisfy the original safety requirement that generated Remotion module lanes never execute in the browser.

Existing dirty files include `.megaplan/schemas/*`, `SequenceCreatorPanel.tsx`, `SequenceParamEditor.tsx`, and `ClipPanel.sequence.test.tsx`. Treat those as current workspace state and do not touch unrelated `.megaplan` schema dirtiness.

## Phase 1: Trusted V1 Authoring UX And Request Contract

### Step 1: Add explicit intent capture to `SequenceCreatorPanel` (`src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/SequenceCreatorPanel.tsx`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add an optional freeform `animationIntent` state beside `prompt` and `editPrompt` for motion, timing, reuse, structure, and style notes.
2. Keep the existing prompt as the primary authoring control. Fixed motion presets remain editable simple-mode params on generated drafts, not a required pre-generation UX.
3. Send intent to `ai-generate-sequence` as `animation_intent: { freeform: animationIntent.trim() }` only when non-empty, while keeping `allowed_clip_types: AVAILABLE_SEQUENCE_CLIP_TYPES` unchanged.
4. In edit mode, send the same top-level `animation_intent` and include the selected source draft in `edit_context` as today, so edits can refine motion and asset reuse without broadening the trusted draft schema.

### Step 2: Preserve lane/provenance metadata on inserted trusted clips (`src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/SequenceCreatorPanel.tsx`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Extend `buildSequenceGenerationMetadata()` so inserted and replaced trusted clips get `generation.sequence_lane: 'trusted_v1'` plus the existing `sequence_creator` prompt/name/draft index metadata.
2. Include sanitized freeform intent in `generation.sequence_creator.intent` when provided. Do not persist model source, imports, raw URLs, or animation refs.
3. Test the panel-level attach path, not only lower-level row builders: generate a trusted draft, click Insert, and assert `applyEdit()` receives `metaUpdates[clipId].generation.sequence_lane === 'trusted_v1'` with prompt/intent provenance.
4. Add the same panel-level assertion for Replace, because insert and replace both call `attachSequenceGenerationMetadata()` but through separate handlers.

### Step 3: Keep repeated asset reuse visible as counts (`src/tools/video-editor/components/PropertiesPanel/SequenceParamEditor.tsx`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Preserve the existing counted-chip behavior for repeated asset keys and ensure the editable input does not render duplicate asset labels as spam.
2. Add a focused test for `imageAssetKeys: ['asset-a', 'asset-a', 'asset-b']` showing two displayed asset chips with `asset-a x2`, while the underlying params still preserve the repeated key array for render materialization.
3. If manual re-entry deduplicates keys through the comma input, keep that behavior explicit and limited to manual edits; generated/model-provided repeated keys must remain visible and persisted until the user changes the field.

## Phase 2: Trusted Draft Validation And Edge Prompting

### Step 4: Keep trusted draft validation closed (`src/tools/video-editor/sequences/validation.ts`, `supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence/sequence-validation.ts`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Keep the trusted draft output shape data-only: `clipType`, `hold`, and `params` only.
2. Continue rejecting top-level `animation`, `entrance`, `exit`, `transition`, `imports`, `source`, `code`, raw URLs, and arbitrary component refs in browser and edge validators.
3. Keep freeform intent outside the draft object and attach it only as editor-side trusted provenance after the draft has validated.
4. Do not add schema-sequence or generated-module authoring in this phase.

### Step 5: Teach `ai-generate-sequence` about intent while keeping validation closed (`supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence/templates.ts`, `supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence/index.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add `animationIntent?: unknown` to `BuildGenerateSequenceMessagesInput` and include it in the JSON user message as `animation_intent`.
2. Update the system prompt to treat freeform intent as guidance for selecting trusted clip types, timing, asset reuse, and safe string params, not as permission to emit code or unsupported animation references.
3. Plumb `body.animation_intent` from the edge entrypoint into `buildGenerateSequenceMessages()`.
4. Update edge tests to assert the Anthropic user message contains `animation_intent`, the trusted allowlist remains present, and unsafe drafts are still returned only as validation errors without raw unsafe values.

## Phase 3: Generated-Lane Render Contract And Live Safety Boundary

### Step 6: Add an explicit blocked render-route contract (`src/tools/video-editor/lib/renderRouter.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Extend `RouterClipShape` with optional `generation?: { sequence_lane?: unknown; artifact_id?: unknown; artifact_version?: unknown; compiler_version?: unknown }`.
2. Extend `RenderRoute` to include a non-rendering route, e.g. `RenderRoute = 'client' | 'banodoco' | 'blocked'`.
3. Extend `RenderRouteDecision.reason` with typed generated-lane reasons such as `generated_remotion_module`, `remotion_module_missing_artifact`, and `remotion_module_artifact_invalid` if artifact validation state is represented locally.
4. Add `isGeneratedRemotionModuleClip()` and inspect `clip.generation.sequence_lane` before theme-package or native clip-type checks.
5. Return `route: 'blocked'` for `sequence_lane === 'remotion_module'` with missing, empty, or non-string `artifact_id`; never return `client` for malformed module-lane clips.
6. Return the worker route (`banodoco`) with `reason: 'generated_remotion_module'` for valid module-lane clips with a non-empty string `artifact_id`, regardless of whether `clipType` is unknown, native, or registered theme-package.
7. Keep unknown `clipType` without module lane on the existing client loud-placeholder fallback.
8. Ensure `trusted_v1`, `schema_sequence`, unknown lane strings, null, and absent lane metadata do not trigger generated-module routing by themselves.

### Step 7: Wire route decisions into the live render entrypoint (`src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useRenderState.ts`, render-button caller in `src/tools/video-editor/components/VideoEditorShell.tsx` or `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useTimelineState.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Replace the direct `startRender = useClientRender(...)` exposure with a routed `startRender` wrapper that calls `decideRenderRoute(resolvedConfig)` at click time.
2. For `route: 'client'`, call the existing `useClientRender()` callback exactly as before.
3. For `route: 'blocked'`, set `renderStatus` to `error`, set a clear `renderLog` message containing the route reason, and do not call `useClientRender()`.
4. For `route: 'banodoco'`, do not call `useClientRender()`. Use the existing `buildRenderTimelinePayload()` and `enqueueBanodocoRenderTimeline()` path if the hook/caller already has the required `timelineId`, `projectId`, `assetRegistry`, user JWT, and orchestrator URL available; otherwise return a structured render error stating that worker render dispatch is unavailable in this UI context.
5. If adding those runtime inputs is small and already available in `useTimelineState` or `VideoEditorShell`, thread them into `useRenderState()` with minimal signature changes. Do not invent new persistence, artifact APIs, or browser execution fallbacks.
6. Add a focused hook test proving malformed `remotion_module` clips are blocked at `useRenderState().startRender()` and the mocked client renderer is not invoked.
7. Add a focused hook test proving valid `remotion_module` clips do not call the client renderer; they either enqueue through the worker path when required context is provided or produce the explicit worker-unavailable error when context is absent.

### Step 8: Keep persistence additive and schema-compatible (`src/tools/video-editor/types/index.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/serialize.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/timeline-data.ts`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Confirm no migration is required: existing clips may omit `clip.generation` entirely.
2. If adding local types, define additive sequence generation metadata without changing `TimelineConfig` storage fields away from config plus asset registry.
3. Do not hand-edit generated shared schema artifacts. If the local shared `@banodoco/timeline-schema` source cannot already represent the optional generation fields needed for this unlock, update its source/generator in the shared package and regenerate outputs in the owning package; otherwise leave generated artifacts untouched.
4. Add a round-trip test showing legacy clips without `clip.generation` still load, serialize, route, and render through the same native client path as before.

## Phase 4: Focused Tests And Verification

### Step 9: Add focused unit coverage
**Scope:** Medium
1. `src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/SequenceCreatorPanel.test.tsx`: request body includes freeform intent, trusted allowlist remains present, Insert attaches `trusted_v1` provenance, and Replace attaches `trusted_v1` provenance.
2. `src/tools/video-editor/components/PropertiesPanel/ClipPanel.sequence.test.tsx` or a new `SequenceParamEditor` test: repeated asset keys render as counts, not duplicate chips.
3. `supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence/index.test.ts`: prompt includes intent but output remains closed and unsafe drafts are rejected.
4. `src/tools/video-editor/lib/renderRouter.test.ts`: valid `remotion_module` lane routes to worker by lane metadata; missing/empty/non-string `artifact_id` blocks; registered theme clip with module lane still uses generated-module reason; unknown clipType without lane remains client fallback; `trusted_v1` and `schema_sequence` do not trigger module routing.
5. `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useRenderState.test.ts` or the nearest existing hook test: live `startRender()` blocks malformed module-lane clips and never invokes the mocked `useClientRender()` callback; valid module-lane clips also never invoke client rendering.
6. `src/tools/video-editor/lib/timeline-data.test.ts`: lane/provenance metadata round-trips when present, and no-generation legacy clips still round-trip.

### Step 10: Run cheapest validation first, then broader checks
**Scope:** Small
1. Run targeted router and render-entrypoint tests first: `npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.config.ts src/tools/video-editor/lib/renderRouter.test.ts src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useRenderState.test.ts`.
2. Run targeted UI/editor tests: `npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.config.ts src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/SequenceCreatorPanel.test.tsx src/tools/video-editor/lib/timeline-data.test.ts` plus the repeated-asset UI test file.
3. Run edge unit coverage: `npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.edge.config.ts supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence/index.test.ts`.
4. Run `npm run lint` after focused tests pass.
5. Finish with `npm test` if runtime allows.

## Execution Order
1. Implement the router type contract first: add `blocked` and generated-lane reasons in `renderRouter.ts`, then update router tests.
2. Wire `decideRenderRoute()` into `useRenderState().startRender()` before touching UI polish, because this is the live safety boundary.
3. Add hook-level render-entrypoint tests proving malformed and valid module-lane clips never call `useClientRender()`.
4. Add SequenceCreator request/provenance plumbing, keeping trusted v1 as the default.
5. Update edge prompt plumbing and tests after the client request shape is known.
6. Finish with repeated-asset display tests and any small UI adjustments needed by current dirty state.
7. Avoid `.megaplan/` schema files entirely unless the harness itself changes them.

## Validation Order
1. Render-router tests first.
2. Live render-entrypoint tests second.
3. SequenceCreator and repeated-asset UI tests third.
4. Edge function unit tests fourth.
5. Lint/typecheck and broader `npm test` last.

      "id": "DEBT-041",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the actual export surface in `supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/llm/messages.ts` and its consumers. step 6 says to 'remove local `isrecord`' from `messages.ts` and import it from `utils.ts`, but without also preserving `messages.ts` as an exporter or rewiring `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, that change would break the module graph immediately.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "cleanup-and-quality-pass-on-20260404-0138"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-055",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the render-gating cleanup plan against remotion's runtime exports in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/cancel-render.js:46-70`. `cancelrender()` stores an error and throws to abort the render; it is not a benign cleanup for an abandoned `delayrender` handle, so step 2's instruction to call `cancelrender(handle)` on unmount is technically wrong and would abort preview or export with an error.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "add-audio-reactive-effects-to-20260406-0440"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-056",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked how remotion itself acquires delay handles in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/iframe.js:8-18`. remotion's own components create the handle synchronously with `usestate(() => delayrender(...))`; the plan says 'calls delayrender() on mount', which reads like an effect-time action and leaves the initial-frame blocking behavior underspecified compared with the actual library pattern.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "add-audio-reactive-effects-to-20260406-0440"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-057",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new `useaudioparam()` sketch against `src/tools/video-editor/effects/compileeffect.tsx:1-2`. the plan defines it in terms of `interpolate(audio[binding.source], ...)` but does not explicitly import `interpolate` into the new hook file or simplify it to straight arithmetic, so the helper's implementation contract is still looser than it needs to be.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "add-audio-reactive-effects-to-20260406-0440"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-070",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked revised step 2.4 against `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useclipresize.ts:76-100`. the plan still says to clamp audio by writing back to `action.end`, but the current hook treats the callback `action` as input data and then creates fresh row actions from it. mutating that caller-owned object is unnecessary and risks coupling the resize hook to outside references instead of using a local `clampedend` variable.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-video-and-audio-clip-20260406-0616"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-076",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the no-duration-fetch decision against `src/tools/travel-between-images/hooks/video/useshotfinalvideos.ts:19-37`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/config-utils.ts:17-35`, and `src/tools/video-editor/compositions/visualclip.tsx:114-123`. the repository still derives clip length from `to - from`, and the final-video query still provides no duration metadata, so the revised plan still cannot prove that `to: endtime - starttime` is safe for shorter source media; a short final video can still be asked to play longer than its actual content.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "add-a-final-video-available-20260406-0801"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-077",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the hand-built replacement clip against the existing insert path in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useassetmanagement.ts:201-252,262-271` and serialization in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts:210-216`. the revised `timelineeditor` sketch still bypasses current insertion invariants such as manual-track geometry defaults and explicit `cliporder` updates, so replacement clips on manual visual tracks will not be initialized the same way as other inserted video clips and may serialize in a different per-track order.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "add-a-final-video-available-20260406-0801"
      ]
    },

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-038",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the current callers of `isrecord` from `llm/messages.ts`. `index.ts` uses it to validate `rawsession`, `db.ts` uses it on db rows, `tool-calls.ts` uses it on parsed tool-call payloads and nested `function` objects, and `selectedclips.ts` uses it while normalizing attachments, so preserving that import contract matters to four different call paths.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "cleanup-and-quality-pass-on-20260404-0138"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-095",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the current `commitdata()` and history pipeline treat each `applyedit()` as its own commit boundary. because step 16 proposes a post-commit reconcile edit after deletions, the actual callers here matter: without `skiphistory` or transaction coalescing, one user action will produce two history events and likely two saves.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-096",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: `handleassetdrop()` and the external-drop wrapper are not pure builders; they immediately mutate editor state and call `applyedit()` per asset. that means the plan cannot literally reuse those callers for atomic shot-drop creation without first extracting a lower-level helper, or it will violate its own \"single applyedit\" requirement.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-111",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the actual commit/history callers around step 18. `commitdata()` emits `beforecommit` and schedules save on every edit, and `usetimelinehistory.onbeforecommit()` pushes undo entries per commit boundary, so a reconcile-after-delete `applyedit` still fragments one user action into multiple history/save operations unless the plan also changes those caller contracts.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-115",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the multi-drag callers in `useclipdrag`. the 'new track' grouped-drag branch still routes through `buildconfigfromdragresult()` and `applyedit({ type: 'config' })`, so the revised plan still does not fully handle every caller shape for grouped moves once pinned-group metadata becomes part of the timeline state.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-130",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the `usepinnedgroupsync()` call site in `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx:479` against the proposed context-based interaction guard. because sync is called directly from `timelineeditor`, routing its guard through new editor-context plumbing is not the most direct fit for the actual caller and depends on extra provider/type changes that the step does not enumerate.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "refactor-pinned-shot-groups-20260408-0508"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-138",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the caller contract of `useselectedmediaclips()` via [usetimelineeditordata](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useselectedmediaclips.ts#l29). this hook's caller currently provides `selectedclipids` and `resolvedconfig`, not a resolved config object with `pinnedshotgroups`, so the plan still does not handle the actual arguments passed at that call site and needs to pull pinned groups from editor `data` instead.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-first-class-shot-20260409-2052"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-158",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the actual callers of the timeline-selection refs with `rg 'selectedclipidsref|additiveselectionref|selectedclipidref|selectedtrackidref' src/tools/video-editor`. save/poll paths consume `selectedclipidref` and `selectedtrackidref`, but `useclipdrag.ts`, `timelineeditor.tsx`, `videoeditorprovider.tsx`, and `usetimelinestate.contexts.ts` also consume `selectedclipidsref` and `additiveselectionref`, so the plan does not yet handle all caller shapes unless it adds a store-backed adapter for the multi-select refs as well.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1b-from-docs-state-20260416-1226"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-159",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked `setselectedclipid(` callers in `useassetmanagement.ts`, `usecliptextoverlay.ts`, `propertiespanel.tsx`, and `previewpanel.tsx`. those callers pass concrete clip ids and `null` through the existing dispatch-like api, so any store action that replaces `setselectedclipid` must preserve the same semantics for clearing and reselection rather than narrowing the contract to a simpler single-purpose setter.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1b-from-docs-state-20260416-1226"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-175",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the real callers of the safe timeline hooks in `src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/useaddtovideoeditor.ts`. that code passes through both mounted-editor values and the `null` outside-provider case, then uses `ops.registergenerationasset`, `ops.handleassetdrop`, and `data.resolvedconfig?.clips` when available, so any replacement api has to preserve both nullable access and the current immediate-drop contract rather than only exposing non-null store selectors.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1c-from-docs-state-20260416-2053"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-176",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelinecanvas.tsx` as a caller of `usetimelineeditordata()`. it only reads `dataref`, but it passes that mutable ref straight into `useclipresizegesture`, which relies on latest timeline data during pointer sequences; the plan does not yet say how this exact caller will receive a stable mutable adapter once the context is removed.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1c-from-docs-state-20260416-2053"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-177",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked `src/tools/video-editor/components/videoeditorshell.tsx`, where `handlekeyboarddelete` combines `editordata.dataref.current` with `editordata.selectedclipids` to build a mutation before falling back to `handledeleteclips`. that caller needs synchronized latest-ref and latest-selection semantics, so splitting those reads across unrelated store selectors without an adapter can change behavior even if each field is individually correct.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "milestone-m1c-from-docs-state-20260416-2053"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-188",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the actual attachment merge callers: `agentchat.tsx` currently passes timeline clips from `useselectedmediaclips()` and gallery clips from `usegalleryselection().selectedgalleryclips` into the merge. because both caller paths supply `clipid`, a `clipid || url` key handles placeholder timeline clips but no longer matches the real gallery-vs-timeline same-url caller case that the old url-keyed merge handled.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "selection-coordination-20260426-1613"
      ]
    }

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```

        # Implementation Plan: First Practical Generated Sequence Unlock

## Overview
The current Reigh implementation already has the trusted v1 spine the architecture doc wants to preserve: `SequenceCreatorPanel` calls `ai-generate-sequence`, validates returned data-only drafts, previews trusted drafts through existing Remotion components, and inserts ordinary timeline clips through `sequence-drafts`. Timeline persistence already preserves `params`, `pool_id`, `clip_order`, `source_uuid`, and `generation` metadata through `TimelineConfig` round-trips.

The critiques show the root cause is broader than the initial router helper target. Generated-lane safety must cover every browser execution surface, not just export/render start. The live render button currently goes through `useRenderState()` to `useClientRender()`, while editor preview goes through `RemotionPreview` to `TimelineRenderer`. The revised approach keeps the render-entrypoint fix and adds lane-aware preview blocking before any native, sequence, or theme `clipType` dispatch. This is still within the original scope because the user explicitly required future generated Remotion module lanes without executing untrusted code in the browser.

Existing dirty files include `.megaplan/schemas/*`, `SequenceCreatorPanel.tsx`, `SequenceParamEditor.tsx`, and `ClipPanel.sequence.test.tsx`. Treat those as current workspace state and do not touch unrelated `.megaplan` schema dirtiness.

## Phase 1: Trusted V1 Authoring UX And Request Contract

### Step 1: Add explicit intent capture to `SequenceCreatorPanel` (`src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/SequenceCreatorPanel.tsx`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add an optional freeform `animationIntent` state beside `prompt` and `editPrompt` for motion, timing, reuse, structure, and style notes.
2. Keep the existing prompt as the primary authoring control. Fixed motion presets remain editable simple-mode params on generated drafts, not a required pre-generation UX.
3. Send intent to `ai-generate-sequence` as `animation_intent: { freeform: animationIntent.trim() }` only when non-empty, while keeping `allowed_clip_types: AVAILABLE_SEQUENCE_CLIP_TYPES` unchanged.
4. In edit mode, send the same top-level `animation_intent` and include the selected source draft in `edit_context` as today, so edits can refine motion and asset reuse without broadening the trusted draft schema.

### Step 2: Preserve lane/provenance metadata on inserted trusted clips (`src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/SequenceCreatorPanel.tsx`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Extend `buildSequenceGenerationMetadata()` so inserted and replaced trusted clips get `generation.sequence_lane: 'trusted_v1'` plus the existing `sequence_creator` prompt/name/draft index metadata.
2. Include sanitized freeform intent in `generation.sequence_creator.intent` when provided. Do not persist model source, imports, raw URLs, or animation refs.
3. Test the panel-level attach path: generate a trusted draft, click Insert, and assert `applyEdit()` receives `metaUpdates[clipId].generation.sequence_lane === 'trusted_v1'` with prompt/intent provenance.
4. Add the same panel-level assertion for Replace, because insert and replace both call `attachSequenceGenerationMetadata()` but through separate handlers.

### Step 3: Keep repeated asset reuse visible as counts (`src/tools/video-editor/components/PropertiesPanel/SequenceParamEditor.tsx`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Preserve the existing counted-chip behavior for repeated asset keys and ensure the editable input does not render duplicate asset labels as spam.
2. Add a focused test for `imageAssetKeys: ['asset-a', 'asset-a', 'asset-b']` showing two displayed asset chips with `asset-a x2`, while the underlying params still preserve the repeated key array for render materialization.
3. If manual re-entry deduplicates keys through the comma input, keep that behavior explicit and limited to manual edits; generated/model-provided repeated keys must remain visible and persisted until the user changes the field.

## Phase 2: Trusted Draft Validation And Edge Prompting

### Step 4: Keep trusted draft validation closed (`src/tools/video-editor/sequences/validation.ts`, `supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence/sequence-validation.ts`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Keep the trusted draft output shape data-only: `clipType`, `hold`, and `params` only.
2. Continue rejecting top-level `animation`, `entrance`, `exit`, `transition`, `imports`, `source`, `code`, raw URLs, and arbitrary component refs in browser and edge validators.
3. Keep freeform intent outside the draft object and attach it only as editor-side trusted provenance after the draft has validated.
4. Do not add schema-sequence or generated-module authoring in this phase.

### Step 5: Teach `ai-generate-sequence` about intent while keeping validation closed (`supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence/templates.ts`, `supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence/index.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add `animationIntent?: unknown` to `BuildGenerateSequenceMessagesInput` and include it in the JSON user message as `animation_intent`.
2. Update the system prompt to treat freeform intent as guidance for selecting trusted clip types, timing, asset reuse, and safe string params, not as permission to emit code or unsupported animation references.
3. Plumb `body.animation_intent` from the edge entrypoint into `buildGenerateSequenceMessages()`.
4. Update edge tests to assert the Anthropic user message contains `animation_intent`, the trusted allowlist remains present, and unsafe drafts are still returned only as validation errors without raw unsafe values.

## Phase 3: Generated-Lane Contracts And Browser Safety Boundaries

### Step 6: Add shared generated-lane helpers (`src/tools/video-editor/lib/generated-lanes.ts`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Create a small shared helper module for lane metadata checks so router, render entrypoint, and preview renderer use one definition.
2. Define a narrow metadata shape: `generation?: { sequence_lane?: unknown; artifact_id?: unknown; artifact_version?: unknown; compiler_version?: unknown }`.
3. Export `isGeneratedRemotionModuleClip(clip)` and `getGeneratedRemotionModuleArtifactId(clip)` or equivalent helpers that require `sequence_lane === 'remotion_module'` and a non-empty string `artifact_id` for valid module clips.
4. Keep `trusted_v1`, `schema_sequence`, unknown lane strings, null, and absent lane metadata non-module by default.

### Step 7: Add an explicit blocked render-route contract (`src/tools/video-editor/lib/renderRouter.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Use the shared generated-lane helpers before theme-package or native `clipType` checks.
2. Extend `RenderRoute` to include a non-rendering route, e.g. `RenderRoute = 'client' | 'banodoco' | 'blocked'`.
3. Extend `RenderRouteDecision.reason` with typed generated-lane reasons such as `generated_remotion_module`, `remotion_module_missing_artifact`, and `remotion_module_artifact_invalid` if artifact validation state is represented locally.
4. Return `route: 'blocked'` for `sequence_lane === 'remotion_module'` with missing, empty, or non-string `artifact_id`; never return `client` for malformed module-lane clips.
5. Return the worker route (`banodoco`) with `reason: 'generated_remotion_module'` for valid module-lane clips with a non-empty string `artifact_id`, regardless of whether `clipType` is unknown, native, or registered theme-package.
6. Keep unknown `clipType` without module lane on the existing client loud-placeholder fallback.

### Step 8: Wire route decisions into the live render entrypoint (`src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useRenderState.ts`, render-button caller in `src/tools/video-editor/components/VideoEditorShell.tsx` or `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useTimelineState.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Replace the direct `startRender = useClientRender(...)` exposure with a routed `startRender` wrapper that calls `decideRenderRoute(resolvedConfig)` at click time.
2. For `route: 'client'`, call the existing `useClientRender()` callback exactly as before.
3. For `route: 'blocked'`, set `renderStatus` to `error`, set a clear `renderLog` message containing the route reason, and do not call `useClientRender()`.
4. For `route: 'banodoco'`, do not call `useClientRender()`. Use the existing `buildRenderTimelinePayload()` and `enqueueBanodocoRenderTimeline()` path if the hook/caller already has the required `timelineId`, `projectId`, `assetRegistry`, user JWT, and orchestrator URL available; otherwise return a structured render error stating that worker render dispatch is unavailable in this UI context.
5. If adding those runtime inputs is small and already available in `useTimelineState` or `VideoEditorShell`, thread them into `useRenderState()` with minimal signature changes. Do not invent new persistence, artifact APIs, or browser execution fallbacks.
6. Add a focused hook test proving malformed `remotion_module` clips are blocked at `useRenderState().startRender()` and the mocked client renderer is not invoked.
7. Add a focused hook test proving valid `remotion_module` clips do not call the client renderer; they either enqueue through the worker path when required context is provided or produce the explicit worker-unavailable error when context is absent.

### Step 9: Block generated-module clips in browser preview before `clipType` dispatch (`src/tools/video-editor/compositions/TimelineRenderer.tsx`, `src/tools/video-editor/components/PreviewPanel/RemotionPreview.tsx`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Treat editor preview as a browser execution surface. The safety boundary must apply before `TimelineRenderer` renders native media, text, registered sequence/theme clips, or unknown placeholders.
2. In `TimelineRenderer`, check `clip.generation.sequence_lane === 'remotion_module'` at the top of the per-clip render path, before any native, sequence, or theme `clipType` dispatch.
3. For module-lane clips, render a non-executing placeholder/block component that shows a safe message such as “Generated module preview is worker-only” and optionally the artifact id/version if present. Do not pass module source, props intended for generated code, imports, or raw source text into browser components.
4. Keep trusted v1, schema-sequence compiled ordinary clips, native media/text, and registered theme-package preview behavior unchanged when they do not carry `sequence_lane === 'remotion_module'`.
5. Prefer the `TimelineRenderer` guard as the primary enforcement point because all `RemotionPreview` callers flow through it; only add `RemotionPreview` filtering if needed for clearer tests or user messaging.
6. Add tests proving a module-lane clip with a native `clipType`, a registered sequence/theme `clipType`, and an unknown `clipType` all render the safe placeholder rather than the normal browser preview path.
7. Add regression tests proving trusted v1 sequence clips and legacy clips without `generation` still preview normally.

### Step 10: Keep persistence additive and schema-compatible (`src/tools/video-editor/types/index.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/serialize.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/timeline-data.ts`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Confirm no migration is required: existing clips may omit `clip.generation` entirely.
2. If adding local types, define additive sequence generation metadata without changing `TimelineConfig` storage fields away from config plus asset registry.
3. Do not hand-edit generated shared schema artifacts. If the local shared `@banodoco/timeline-schema` source cannot already represent the optional generation fields needed for this unlock, update its source/generator in the shared package and regenerate outputs in the owning package; otherwise leave generated artifacts untouched.
4. Add a round-trip test showing legacy clips without `clip.generation` still load, serialize, route, preview, and render through the same native client path as before.

## Phase 4: Focused Tests And Verification

### Step 11: Add focused unit coverage
**Scope:** Medium
1. `src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/SequenceCreatorPanel.test.tsx`: request body includes freeform intent, trusted allowlist remains present, Insert attaches `trusted_v1` provenance, and Replace attaches `trusted_v1` provenance.
2. `src/tools/video-editor/components/PropertiesPanel/ClipPanel.sequence.test.tsx` or a new `SequenceParamEditor` test: repeated asset keys render as counts, not duplicate chips.
3. `supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence/index.test.ts`: prompt includes intent but output remains closed and unsafe drafts are rejected.
4. `src/tools/video-editor/lib/renderRouter.test.ts`: valid `remotion_module` lane routes to worker by lane metadata; missing/empty/non-string `artifact_id` blocks; registered theme clip with module lane still uses generated-module reason; unknown clipType without lane remains client fallback; `trusted_v1` and `schema_sequence` do not trigger module routing.
5. `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useRenderState.test.ts` or the nearest existing hook test: live `startRender()` blocks malformed module-lane clips and never invokes the mocked `useClientRender()` callback; valid module-lane clips also never invoke client rendering.
6. `src/tools/video-editor/compositions/TimelineRenderer.sequence.test.tsx` or a focused renderer test: browser preview blocks `remotion_module` clips before native, registered, or unknown `clipType` dispatch, and trusted v1/legacy clips still preview normally.
7. `src/tools/video-editor/lib/timeline-data.test.ts`: lane/provenance metadata round-trips when present, and no-generation legacy clips still round-trip.

### Step 12: Run cheapest validation first, then broader checks
**Scope:** Small
1. Run targeted router and render-entrypoint tests first: `npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.config.ts src/tools/video-editor/lib/renderRouter.test.ts src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useRenderState.test.ts`.
2. Run targeted preview safety tests: `npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.config.ts src/tools/video-editor/compositions/TimelineRenderer.sequence.test.tsx src/tools/video-editor/components/PreviewPanel/RemotionPreview.test.tsx` using only files that exist or are added.
3. Run targeted UI/editor tests: `npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.config.ts src/tools/video-editor/components/SequenceCreator/SequenceCreatorPanel.test.tsx src/tools/video-editor/lib/timeline-data.test.ts` plus the repeated-asset UI test file.
4. Run edge unit coverage: `npx vitest run --config config/testing/vitest.edge.config.ts supabase/functions/ai-generate-sequence/index.test.ts`.
5. Run `npm run lint` after focused tests pass.
6. Finish with `npm test` if runtime allows.

## Execution Order
1. Implement shared generated-lane helpers first so router, render entrypoint, and preview renderer use one lane definition.
2. Implement the router type contract: add `blocked` and generated-lane reasons in `renderRouter.ts`, then update router tests.
3. Wire `decideRenderRoute()` into `useRenderState().startRender()` because this is the live render-button safety boundary.
4. Add lane-aware preview blocking in `TimelineRenderer` before any `clipType` dispatch because editor preview is also a browser execution surface.
5. Add hook-level and renderer-level tests proving malformed and valid module-lane clips never call client render or normal browser preview paths.
6. Add SequenceCreator request/provenance plumbing, keeping trusted v1 as the default.
7. Update edge prompt plumbing and tests after the client request shape is known.
8. Finish with repeated-asset display tests and any small UI adjustments needed by current dirty state.
9. Avoid `.megaplan/` schema files entirely unless the harness itself changes them.

## Validation Order
1. Render-router tests first.
2. Live render-entrypoint tests second.
3. Browser preview safety tests third.
4. SequenceCreator and repeated-asset UI tests fourth.
5. Edge function unit tests fifth.
6. Lint/typecheck and broader `npm test` last.

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-038",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the current callers of `isrecord` from `llm/messages.ts`. `index.ts` uses it to validate `rawsession`, `db.ts` uses it on db rows, `tool-calls.ts` uses it on parsed tool-call payloads and nested `function` objects, and `selectedclips.ts` uses it while normalizing attachments, so preserving that import contract matters to four different call paths.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "cleanup-and-quality-pass-on-20260404-0138"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-095",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the current `commitdata()` and history pipeline treat each `applyedit()` as its own commit boundary. because step 16 proposes a post-commit reconcile edit after deletions, the actual callers here matter: without `skiphistory` or transaction coalescing, one user action will produce two history events and likely two saves.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-096",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: `handleassetdrop()` and the external-drop wrapper are not pure builders; they immediately mutate editor state and call `applyedit()` per asset. that means the plan cannot literally reuse those callers for atomic shot-drop creation without first extracting a lower-level helper, or it will violate its own \"single applyedit\" requirement.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-111",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the actual commit/history callers around step 18. `commitdata()` emits `beforecommit` and schedules save on every edit, and `usetimelinehistory.onbeforecommit()` pushes undo entries per commit boundary, so a reconcile-after-delete `applyedit` still fragments one user action into multiple history/save operations unless the plan also changes those caller contracts.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-115",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the multi-drag callers in `useclipdrag`. the 'new track' grouped-drag branch still routes through `buildconfigfromdragresult()` and `applyedit({ type: 'config' })`, so the revised plan still does not fully handle every caller shape for grouped moves once pinned-group metadata becomes part of the timeline state.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-130",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the `usepinnedgroupsync()` call site in `src/tools/video-editor/components/timelineeditor/timelineeditor.tsx:479` against the proposed context-based interaction guard. because sync is called directly from `timelineeditor`, routing its guard through new editor-context plumbing is not the most direct fit for the actual caller and depends on extra provider/type changes that the step does not enumerate.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "refactor-pinned-shot-groups-20260408-0508"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-138",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the caller contract of `useselectedmediaclips()` via [usetimelineeditordata](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useselectedmediaclips.ts#l29). this hook's caller currently provides `selectedclipids` and `resolvedconfig`, not a resolved config object with `pinnedshotgroups`, so the plan still does not handle the actual arguments passed at that call site and needs to pull pinned groups from editor `data` instead.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

      "id": "DEBT-158",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the actual callers of the timeline-selection refs with `rg 'selectedclipidsref|additiveselectionref|selectedclipidref|selectedtrackidref' src/tools/video-editor`. save/poll paths consume `selectedclipidref` and `selectedtrackidref`, but `useclipdrag.ts`, `timelineeditor.tsx`, `videoeditorprovider.tsx`, and `usetimelinestate.contexts.ts` also consume `selectedclipidsref` and `additiveselectionref`, so the plan does not yet handle all caller shapes unless it adds a store-backed adapter for the multi-select refs as well.",

      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked `setselectedclipid(` callers in `useassetmanagement.ts`, `usecliptextoverlay.ts`, `propertiespanel.tsx`, and `previewpanel.tsx`. those callers pass concrete clip ids and `null` through the existing dispatch-like api, so any store action that replaces `setselectedclipid` must preserve the same semantics for clearing and reselection rather than narrowing the contract to a simpler single-purpose setter.",
